The other 63 are pinned with reasons in `tests/governance_v4/template_coverage_baseline.txt` and gated by `test_template_coverage.sh`. **Read but inert (open — wire or delete):** `code_quality.no_hallucinated_apis.{python,javascript,shell,go,ruby,cross_language}_patterns` (the check uses built-in tables; verified for python/shell/cross_language with `custom_patterns` blocking on the same marker and block); `code_quality.no_hardcoded_{urls,ips}.{patterns,custom_patterns}` (fixed regex; verified against the sibling `no_sql_injection.patterns`, which blocks); `context_drift.weights.coherence_velocity` (traced; `governance.h` marks it INERT); `scoring.green_threshold` and `scorers.<name>.green_threshold` (traced: echoed by `governance.scorer()`, never compared by `zone()`). Already in the template and equally inert (traced): `no_hallucinated_apis.check_made_up_functions` / `check_wrong_syntax`. **Never read at all:** `code_quality.no_hardcoded_{urls,ips}.allowlist`: the check consults `cfg.allowlist`, but the loader never fills it, so an allowlisted host still blocks (verified; fails closed). Annotated NOT READ in the template. Beyond those, `--unread` lists 145 template leaves `loadFromJson()` never reads (SCREENED — e.g. `restrictions.crypto.block_*`, `no_dead_code.detect_*`, `circuit_breaker.critical_coherence`, the per-language `*_level` keys, whose level is set by the check's own value, `"no_var": "advisory"`). Not changed; each needs tracing before deletion. Instrument note: `test_inert_key_sweep.sh` matches only `rules_.X =` assignments, so alias assignments (`ha.python_patterns = ...`) are invisible to it, and it lists `agent_dispatch.default_timeout_seconds` although the loader copies that key into every agent without an explicit `timeout` (another loader-as-consumer false positive). |
| A31 | **Entry-file and imported-module function checks disagree, in both directions.** Found 2026-10-09 while moving the Governance Check workflow to lint module files (#299). (a) **Imports skip the source checks.** The VM scans only the ENTRY file's source for secrets, PII and incomplete logic (`main.cpp`, `checkSecrets(source, 0)` before compile); the tree-walker runs them per function inside `checkNaabFunctionBody()`, which is skipped under module loading (`3e7c94e1`, `ae4bfb67`: done to avoid heuristic false positives, and behavioral contracts were deliberately kept -- nothing in either commit says secret scanning was meant to go too). Measured on both engines: a TODO and a secret planted in a module each block it (exit 3) when it is run standalone, and neither is caught when it is imported -- the importer runs at exit 0. (b) **The entry file skips contracts under a contracts-only config.** `checkNaabFunctionBody()` returns early unless one of seven named checks (oversimplification, incomplete logic, placeholders, complexity floor, plugins, secrets, PII) is enabled, and the contract check sits below that return -- so with `contracts` and no `code_quality` check, a `must_call` breach in the entry file runs at exit 0 on both engines while the same function IMPORTED is blocked (the module path calls `checkFunctionBehavioralContract()` directly). `duplicate_calls` is masked the same way. An outer gate masking an inner one: the early return is a hand-maintained list of what runs below it. | **fixed** 2026-10-09 | Both engines now run `checkNaabSourceText()` (secrets, PII, incomplete logic) on the entry file and every imported module, and a module's functions get `checkNaabFunctionBody(..., imported_module=true)` -- content checks and the contract, not the heuristics (ae4bfb67's split kept). The tree-walker gained the entry-file whole-source check it never had (a top-level `export let` secret ran there). The early return is gone; stripping is lazy. Governance plugins stay exempt. History: the module skip came from `f9dd7e2c`, a performance fix the day after EVA-8 widened the scan for anti-evasion. Still open: stubs and other heuristic findings in a module pass, by `ae4bfb67`'s decision; plugin rules do not run on module functions. Test: `tests/governance_v4/test_module_source_checks.sh`; `test_module_governance_parity.sh` Group A flipped. |
| A32 | **A withheld environment variable, and the attestation signing key, may still be readable by a running program under `elevated`.** Found 2026-10-12 by an external review of #307 (MCP slice 1), then re-measured on that PR's build after its fix. **Observed** (one run, `mode: enforce`, `sandbox_level: elevated`, MCP worker, lease without `ENV_READ`, benign marker value): `env.get()` is refused, embedded Python's `os.environ` and a `<<sh>>` child's environment no longer hold the value (#307's allowlist scrub, arm MC-06e) -- but the scrub stores the popped values in `_naab_saved_env`, a dict in the `__main__` namespace the block itself runs in, so a `<<python>>` block reads them back -- under a lease with no capabilities at all, and in a plain CLI run with `env_vars.blocked_read` set (observed on #307's build; the dict came with #302's scrub, so master is expected to match -- not yet run there). And `/proc/self/environ` still holds the value, read by `file.read`, `io.read_file` and Python `open()` with `FS_READ` granted, and by a `<<sh>>` child reading `/proc/$PPID/environ` with `SHELL_EXEC` granted. `env.load_dotenv("/proc/self/environ")` is fixture-dependent and was measured both ways on master (benign marker, both engines): refused under `env_vars.read:false` (its own "all environment variables" env-read guard), but it LEAKED under `blocked_read:[VAR]`, which that guard does not cover. Separately, `audit.provenance.signing_key` is a protected path for the stdlib (arm MC-14k) but path policy does not reach subprocess languages, so a `<<sh>>` block in a shell-enabled project reads the key (CONTRA-013; reproduced with a scratch key). The protection-map env probes (`tools/protmap/protmap.py`) read only the child's OWN environment, so the map's env cells marked contained since #302 did not cover the `/proc` route. | **fixed** (stdlib + embedded Python); subprocess + signing key documented | Fixed on master (both engines): the filesystem gate now refuses a read of the process environment file (`/proc/<id>/environ`, including the task-level path) whenever an env-read restriction is in force (`env_vars.read:false`, `blocked_read` or `allowed_read` non-empty) -- `isProcessEnvironPath()` in `naab/path_policy.h`, consulted once in `checkPathAccessImpl()`, the single decision point `file`/`io`/`csv`/`path`/`load_dotenv` and the embedded-Python audit hook all reach. It only ever turns PASS into BLOCK and only when the operator set an env policy, so a project with no env policy still reads the file. The embedded-Python `_naab_saved_env` readback is closed by holding the scrub's save dict C-side during user code and re-inserting it explicitly on every exit, while the GIL is still held. A first draft re-inserted it from a destructor, which runs after the executor's explicit GIL release, and `PolyglotAsyncTest.PythonException` (a raising block on a worker thread) segfaulted in CI under Python 3.12. It was reproduced here against 3.12 and fixed; Python 3.11 did not crash. **Remaining, documented `elevated` limit**: a subprocess language (`<<sh>>` reading `/proc/$PPID/environ`) still reaches the file -- path policy does not reach a separate process (CONTRA-013); and the signing-key subprocess read above. Both close with `capabilities.shell.enabled:false` or a lease without `SHELL_EXEC`. A deeper residual: `gc.get_objects()` from embedded Python can still reach the save-dict object. Test: `tests/security/test_env_file_bypass.sh` (positive control per route; revert-checked against the pristine master binary -- 10 gate/saved arms fail there; the `<<sh>>` arm asserts the documented limit is STILL open so it flips if someone closes it without updating this row). |
| A33 | **The governance evidence files were writable by the governed program; two adjacent gaps on the same files are left open.** Found 2026-10-14 tracing the live-guidance slice-2 prerequisite (slice 2 reads previous runs' telemetry as ground truth, so it must be trustworthy first). The main defect -- `telemetry`/`transcript`/`audit` output files writable by the program on every route, both engines -- is **fixed** (see the CLAUDE.md evidence-files entry and `tests/governance_v4/test_evidence_file_protection.sh`). Two adjacent gaps were traced, NOT yet tested or fixed, both on these same files: **(a)** the reload ratchet does not cover `telemetry.output_file`, `transcript.output_file`, `audit.output_file` or `audit.level` -- all four are in `ratchet_unclassified_baseline.txt` -- so a signed reload could move an output file or set `audit.level: "none"` mid-run (grow-only path recording keeps the OLD file protected, but a reload still redirects future evidence writes to a new location, and `audit.level:"none"` stops the audit trail); **(b)** the environment variables named by `telemetry.tamper_evidence.hmac_key_env` and `audit.provenance.signing_key_env` are resolved via `std::getenv` at load and stay readable by the program (only guidance's `auth_env` is withheld today, via `protected_env_names_`) -- so a program that can read the chain's HMAC key could forge a correctly-chained append, which the plan-live-guidance.md slice-2 note already flags as the reason the unkeyed chain detects edits but not a forged append. Owner's decision 2026-10-14: fix the write protection first (done), take (a) and (b) as separate follow-ups each with its own history pass. | **main defect fixed; (a) ratchet fixed (see below); (b) key-env withholding open** | (a) add the three output paths and `audit.level` to `checkCapabilityListRatchet()` (the ratchet-coverage guard in `test_ratchet_coverage.sh` will shrink its baseline by four); an output path should be move-only-to-a-still-protected-location or refused, and `audit.level` ratchets toward MORE logging (`none` < `basic` < `full`). (b) add both key-env names to `protected_env_names_` in `addGovernanceProtectedPaths()` beside guidance's `auth_env`, grow-only, so the program cannot read the key that chains its own evidence. Neither is a prerequisite for the write-protection PR; sequence after it. **(a) FIXED 2026-10-14** (`checkEvidenceRatchet()` in `governance_config.cpp`, test `tests/governance_v4/test_evidence_ratchet.sh`, 41 arms). Reproduced first, signed reload accepted with no violation, against an unrelated reload as the control (21 telemetry lines with `RunEnd`, 4 audit entries): `telemetry.enabled` false left 2 lines and no `RunEnd`; a moved `output_file` split the run across two files; `audit.level: "none"` left 1 entry; clearing `log_events.polyglot_executed` left 1 of 3. The four keys named here were 4 of 37 unclassified evidence keys; all 37 are now compared or classified -- destinations (telemetry, transcript, audit, reports), what is recorded (`telemetry.enabled`, `deduplicate_checks`, `transcript.agents`, `audit.level`, `log_events`, attestations), off-site forwarding (`webhook_url`, its credential, the drop-affecting `forward_*` knobs) and the keys that seal it (the two `hmac_key`s, `signing_key`, compared as CONFIGURED -- a changed value under the same variable name is (b)). Judged by what is recorded, through the writers' predicates: `audit.level` basic -> full, a re-spelled path, and `polyglot_executed` off with `polyglot_timing` on are accepted. Building it found three more, fixed in the same PR: **any** accepted reload, even of the same file, dropped the report and telemetry destinations the CLI lays over govern.json (`--governance-report/-telemetry`, `governance.report_*`, `governance.telemetry`) -- the reload built its rules from govern.json alone (`StartupOutputs`, re-applied by `reloadIfChanged()`); under `--tree-walk` `governance.report_*` and `governance.telemetry` did nothing at all; and a telemetry file named by `--governance-telemetry` was writable by the program (#314 protected only the files govern.json names). The other startup overlays a reload drops are A37. |
| A34 | **Outbound connections took their proxy -- and the operator's their CA bundle -- from the LIVE environment, which a program's `env.set` writes; URL module imports kept a URL-string SSRF check after #229 moved `http.get` to the resolved address.** Opened 2026-10-14 from a finding the #313 session reported without an end-to-end test. Reproduced first, with controls, on `526044de` with the pre-#313 `agent_provider.cpp` and `telemetry_forwarder.cpp` put back: a program that `env.set` `*_proxy` to a closed local port broke the agent call (`Couldn't connect to server`, both engines) and the event the webhook should have carried after the `env.set` never arrived; one that pointed `SSL_CERT_FILE`/`CURL_CA_BUNDLE` at a missing file broke the https agent call (`Problem with the SSL CA cert`) and the https webhook. Controls: every stub answers with the environment left alone, and the same closed proxy / missing CA set BEFORE start does break the call (the probe can fail). The probes never intercept anything -- a closed port and a missing file. So #313's fix was real and needed; this row adds what it lacked. **The class, every outbound connection the engine makes** (enumerated from `curl_easy_init` call sites, plus `connect(`/`getaddrinfo` -- none elsewhere): agent calls (incl. agent review, through `agent_provider.cpp`), the telemetry webhook, guidance sources, the program's `http` module, URL module imports, the package manager. (1) **One snapshot**: #312 (guidance) and #313 (agent/telemetry) had each built their own, captured at different points and disagreeing on upper-case `HTTP_PROXY` (guidance honoured it for http; libcurl and #313 do not, because a CGI host sets it from a request header). #313's fix was also incomplete: it set `CURLOPT_NOPROXY` only when the snapshot had a value, and libcurl reads `no_proxy` from the live environment whenever that option is unset -- measured on the #313 build with the operator's proxy set at start, a program's `env.set("no_proxy", "*")` sent the agent call (both engines) and `http.get` straight past it (EO-06; the call reached the stub, exit 0). Now `env_snapshot.h` holds the one copy, captured at the first config load or when `run` enables guidance, whichever is first; proxy/CA rules follow libcurl and live in one helper (`curl_egress.h`) every site calls. Guidance also used to fall back to the system trust store when the operator's `SSL_CERT_FILE` named a missing file; it now fails like the agent call does (the operator's setting is never silently replaced). (2) **The program's own `http` requests**: their proxy came from the live environment too. Not a credential path, but the host lists judge the URL's HOST while the socket goes to the PROXY -- measured on `526044de`: under `allowed_hosts: ["allowed.test"]`, `http.get("http://allowed.test/")` after `env.set("http_proxy", "http://<unresolvable>.invalid:3128")` failed with `Couldn't resolve proxy name` (curl went to the program's proxy), without it with `Couldn't resolve host name`; an unlisted host was refused in both runs (control). The proxy is now the snapshot's. libcurl does not read `SSL_CERT_FILE` itself (observed: with `CAINFO` unset the operator's bundle was not used), so the http module's CA was never program-steerable and is left at libcurl's default. (3) **URL imports**: measured on `526044de`, enforce, network enabled: an import from `127.0.0.1` refused (the #213 string check) while the same module imported AND RAN from `127.1`, `2130706433` and `0x7f000001`. A23's "FIXED (#213)" covered the literal spellings only; the code comment there said centralising the A11 check would fix both copies, and #229 fixed one. Imports now use #229's gate (`refusePrivateOpenSocket`, moved from `http_impl.cpp` to `curl_egress.cpp`, one copy) whenever governance is active -- the scope of the existing string check -- plus http/https-only on every hop and the snapshot proxy. | **verified, fixed** | Tests: `tests/security/test_operator_egress_env.sh` (EO-00 harness control; EO-01c/EO-03c/EO-05c prove each probe can fail; EO-01/03 agent, EO-02/04 webhook, EO-05 http module, EO-06 `no_proxy`, VM and tree-walker) and `tests/security/test_ssrf_redirect_dns.sh` SI-01..SI-05 (SI-01/SI-04 controls; a refusal needs the target's request log to lack the arm AND the target to still answer, since a dead server also prints `Failed to download`). Revert-checked one mechanism at a time: the agent site reading the live environment fails EO-01/01t/03/03t only; the webhook's fails EO-02/04 only; the http module's fails EO-05/05t only; setting `CURLOPT_NOPROXY` only when non-empty fails EO-06/06t/06h only; guidance's fails C-10 and C-08x; capturing on first use instead of at load fails all eight non-control EO arms; the import gate removed fails SI-02/03/05 with SI-01/04 green. Both suites fail against a dead interpreter. **Traced, NOT fixed or measured here** (each needs a non-private server to stage, so none is testable on loopback): (a) a redirect hop to a host outside `allowed_hosts` -- the host lists are checked on the first URL only, in `http.get` and imports alike; (b) `https_only` on a redirect hop -- both paths allow `http` on redirects; (c) a cached URL import is served without a connection, so the socket gate never sees it (no SSRF in that run -- no connection is made -- but a module cached by an ungoverned run from a loopback spelling imports under governance). **Decision copied, not made**: behind a proxy on a private address (common: a local proxy on 127.0.0.1), #229's gate refuses the PROXY's socket, so `http.get` already failed there; URL imports now fail the same way. A proxy-aware destination check (resolve the target, check it, allow the operator's proxy) would serve both copies -- guidance already does this for its sources (`guidanceSourceAddressForbidden`). |
| A35 | **`capabilities.network.allowed_hosts`/`blocked_hosts` do not reach embedded Python.** Found 2026-10-14 while enumerating outbound connections for A34. The embedded-Python audit hook answers `socket.connect` from the NET_CONNECT capability alone (`python_c_executor.cpp`), never the host lists. | **fixed (Bundle 3)** | Measured on the A34 branch, enforce + elevated, `allowed_hosts: ["listed-host.test"]`: a `<<python>>` `socket.connect(("192.0.2.2", 9))` was ATTEMPTED (`ConnectionRefusedError` from the closed port, not the hook's `PermissionError`), while `http.get("http://192.0.2.2:9/")` in the same program was refused by the sandbox (control). Not a one-line fix, which is why it is not in A34: the hook sees an ADDRESS, the lists hold NAMES, and an allowlist of names cannot judge an IP without a resolution the hook does not have. Options for the owner: judge IP-literal list entries at the hook and refuse any connect when a name allowlist is set (fail closed), or resolve the listed names at load. Subprocess languages are outside host policy as they are outside path policy (CONTRA-013); `capabilities.network.enabled: false` still binds embedded Python. **Premise checked 2026-10-20 (traced, not measured here).** History: A7 (#209, `41bcd62c`) sent `socket.connect` to the sandbox, but the policy asks only `hasCapability(NET_CONNECT)` (`python_c_executor.cpp`), beside the wrapper's comment 'address is a tuple; policy decides on capability' (`python_c_wrapper.c`). The sandbox's host decision, which `http.*` uses, was never called, and nothing records host lists as excluded on purpose. 'The hook sees an ADDRESS' is half right: by CPython's documented audit events, `socket.getaddrinfo(host, ...)` is audited with the NAME before `socket.create_connection` connects, and `socket.connect`'s address carries a name when the caller passed one. NAAb's hook does not subscribe to `getaddrinfo` and reads no connect address. So it can judge the name the program asked for -- the decision `http.get` makes -- at the lookup, and at a connect given a name. The open choice narrows to a connect to an address that an ALLOWED name resolved to: resolve the allowed names in the engine and admit only those addresses, or refuse such connects (fail closed, which breaks `create_connection` to an allowed name under a name allowlist). The lists stay where they are; no new key. **Fixed (Bundle 3, 2026-10-27), by the owner's decision "admit allowed lookups":** the hook now hands every socket event to `naabPythonNetPolicy()` (`python_c_executor.cpp`): `getaddrinfo`/`gethostbyname[_ex]` judge the name (refused when blocked, or when an `allowed_hosts` list does not admit it, or when NET_CONNECT is withheld -- a lookup is a network query); a lookup of an ALLOWED name under an allowlist is resolved by the engine too and its addresses admitted; `connect`/`connect_ex`/`sendto`/`sendmsg` judge a name by `Sandbox::hostPolicyAllows()` (canConnect's host decision, split out so there is one) and an address by an IP entry or an admitted lookup, else refused -- a resolver that answers the program differently fails closed. IP literals are read as getaddrinfo reads them (`2130706433`, `0x7f000001`, `127.1`, the IPv4-mapped form). `gethostbyaddr`/`getnameinfo` are judged without admitting. AF_UNIX paths are not hosts and keep the capability check only. The network switch stays the NET_CONNECT capability it always was, so a run with no govern.json is unchanged (HL-14). Measured before the fix (observed, `scratchpad/a35` probe): with `allowed_hosts` naming another host, TCP connect and UDP sendto to 127.0.0.1 landed; `blocked_hosts ["127.0.0.1"]` connected; **with `network.enabled: false`, a UDP datagram went out through `__import__("so"+"cket")`** (sendto was never judged). Test: `tests/governance_v4/test_python_host_lists.sh` (22 arms; K1, the hook with no network policy registered, fails exactly the 13 refusal arms; 5 mutants). PR On a thread the block spawns, `naabPythonNetPolicy` takes the run-level sandbox through `auditSandbox()`, shared with `naabPythonAuditPolicy` (A85, #343): `test_python_host_lists.sh` HL-15/HL-16 (a spawned thread's connect and UDP send, red without the fallback). |
| A36 | **A guidance command or MCP source runs with the operator's authority, and the program could change what it runs.** Found 2026-10-14 in the adversarial pass on the MCP source. Two routes, both measured with `capabilities.shell.enabled: false` (control: a `<<shell>>` block in the same project is HARD-refused) and `filesystem.mode: "write"`: **(1) rewriting the script** -- `file.write("gen.sh", ...)` over a command source `["./gen.sh"]` succeeded and the next refresh (5 s) ran the program's code, outside the sandbox. #312 skipped command sources in `isGuidanceSourcePath()` ("not a file the program could write" -- the command's program IS a file); an oversight, no decision behind it. **Fixed** with the MCP source: the program a command/MCP source runs (a bare name as the load-time PATH resolves it) and every argument naming an existing file are sources the program may not write (`guidanceSourceProgramFiles()`), refused HARD at the shared write decision point. Tests C-25 / M-17, each with a control that a file beside it stays writable; revert-checked. **(2) a file BESIDE the script** -- with (1) fixed, `file.write("json.py", ...)` next to a command source `["python3", "gen.py"]` succeeded and the next refresh imported it (Python puts the script's directory first on its import path): program code ran as the operator. A per-file block cannot see a NEW file; npm/uv caches and other search paths are the same shape. | **(1) fixed; (2) fixed by the owner's decision (b), 2026-10-14** | **Built:** a command or MCP source is refused (not fetched, its copy dropped, a warning naming the directory and the remedy) whenever the program may write into the directory of its program (when given as a path) or of any argument naming a file -- the filesystem mode allows writes and the path policy refuses no new file there (`guidanceSourceExposure()`, judged on every fetch from the live rules of every attached engine; the sandbox is not consulted, which can only refuse more). Tests C-26 (the measured json.py route end to end: refused, the program's file never runs), C-26c (the same script in a blocked directory is fetched), C-26b (a read-only mode protects it), M-17/M-17c for MCP; revert-checked. **Still outside it:** a program given as a bare name is looked up on PATH and not judged, so what an interpreter or `npx` loads from its own install or package cache is not covered. The options considered were: **(a)** refuse program writes anywhere under the directory of a command/MCP source's program and file arguments -- strong, but a script in the project root would lock the whole project tree; **(b)** refuse at load a command/MCP source whose program or file arguments sit in a directory the program may write (decide-only path check), with the remedy "put it outside the project or under `blocked_paths`" -- fail closed, stops sources that work today; **(c)** a load-time warning only. Recommendation: (b), because the operator then chooses where the protected code lives and nothing about the program's other writes changes. With shell enabled, the program can run code as itself anyway (the operator's grant); this matters where shell is off. |
| A37 | **A reload dropped every startup overlay the engine did not re-apply.** Found 2026-10-14 building A33(a); measured 2026-10-20; **fixed 2026-10-21**. `reloadIfChanged()` built the new rules from govern.json alone, so what the run had laid over them at startup was gone after the first accepted reload or, where the ratchet compares the key, every reload was refused. **Measured before the fix** (observed, `tests/governance_v4/test_reload_startup_overlays.sh` against `445b391e`, a no-op reload, VM unless noted): an `--agent-id` role narrowing languages: every reload REFUSED (`languages`), signed or not, so a tightening reload was refused too (RL-01/03/04); a shell-less role: accepted, the overlay dropped, `<<shell>>` refused by a different check, the per-role one (RS-01); `--env prod` raising audit to enforce: refused (`governance mode`, EP-01); `--env dev` lowering enforce to audit: accepted, and the run was silently back at enforce (ED-01); an environment's report path: refused (`report_json: removed`, ER-01); a CLAUDE.md ban (`project_context`): accepted, and the banned function ran (PC-01); `--governance-check-baselines` (tree-walker): accepted, and a changed output ran (BL-02); **governance plugins**, not on this row's original list: accepted, and no plugin rule ran again, on BOTH engines (PL-01/PL-01v), because `loadPlugins()` marked each plugin loaded inside the rules the reload replaced. Also found: on the tree-walker the baselines flags were applied only when a report flag was given too (BL-01). They had shared an `if` with the report paths since `84034d0b`; setting those paths was the block's original job (`bcf647a0`). History: every overlay predates reload (`596dd798`, whose message names none): never built, not decided. Adjacent observations, unchanged: `--governance-report`, `--governance-sarif` and `--governance-junit` work only after the `run` subcommand (verified; not in the global pre-scan CLAUDE.md requires), and the VM never calls `logPolyglotExecution()`, so `audit.log_events.polyglot_executed` records nothing on the default engine (screened). | **fixed** | One function lays the overlays over every reloaded rule set: `applyStartupOverlays()`, run in startup order, after the minimum levels and before the ratchet. It replays the project-context extractions recorded at startup: the files are read once, so a program that rewrites CLAUDE.md cannot change what a reload applies (PC-02). It re-reads the role and the environment BY NAME from the new file: an operator's edit of either takes effect (ED-02), and a removed environment is reported (EN-01). Then come the output destinations (A33(a)) and the baselines flags, recorded by `setStartupBaselineFlags()`. That is now done on both paths, without the report-flag condition, and the tree-walker also reads `governance.record_/check_baselines` as the VM did. Plugin load state moved out of the rules into the engine (`loaded_plugin_files_`). A reload keeps it, a plugin file the reload adds is loaded on the next check (PL-02), and `loadPlugins()` no longer writes the published rules mid-run. The reload's sandbox sync reads the shell decision from before the overlays, because startup syncs the sandbox before it applies the role. A shell-less role therefore still reaches `checkShellAllowed()` only, and `process.run` behaves after a reload as it did at startup (RS-02); whether it should is A54. The 21 keys that only choose or feed an overlay are classified `startup` in `ratchet_classification.txt` (`governance.agent_id`, `default_env`, `record_/check_baselines`, every `project_context.*`). Revert-checked per mechanism (below in the PR). **Still open, adjacent:** a reload resolves no `extends` (CLAUDE.md, capability-mirrors gotcha); the tree-walker's parallel polyglot path runs no reload check (A55); a tree-walker `async fn` has none of these overlays (A57); output baselines do not work end to end (A56). **The VM half of the adjacent `logPolyglotExecution()` observation is fixed: A81 (Bundle 2).** |
| A38 | **The messages that steer a model toward govern.json had never been mapped as one system.** Mapped 2026-10-15 before adding anything to them (owner's request: live guidance from databases, MCP and HTTP should join a unified helper, "helpful not hurtful"): every channel by reader, its convergence points, output groups and filters, in `docs/message-map.md`. Two passes, the second independent of the first (three enumerations of the code made without sight of pass 1, diffed, each difference verified by a probe). Pass 2 corrected five pass-1 claims -- the placeholder location (a function's start line, not a wrong number), the `Help:` count (313, not about 250), the fatal-path flush (VM only), MCP's block text (absent from `naab_propose`), the tool-result cap (the agent gets the helper intact) -- and added about a dozen items. Defects are A39-A46. **Pass 3 (same day) ran every channel the first two had only traced** -- REST, the REPL, `naab-gov lint`/`check`, the LSP over its stdio protocol, the SARIF/JSON reports, the package manager -- with the same probe program in each, and resolved pass 2's leads. It found two defects in the loosening direction (A45: REST skips every per-function check; A46: the run token budget misses tool-loop calls), extended A39 and A43, and corrected A39(a): the sanitizer's over-redaction was already on record for another of its patterns (`governance-campaign-findings.md`, "`ErrorSanitizer` over-redacts identifiers", pinned by `test_limit_attribution.sh` LA-03), with the constraint that a fix loosens a redaction pattern -- pass 1 and 2 missed that record. Other pass-3 observations: the REPL loads no govern.json (verified: it read the protected `govern.json` that `run` blocks at exit 3) -- **decided**, `docs/GOVERNANCE-LIMITS.md` §5.2, "intentional for interactive development"; its `--repl` governance argument is unused, and its completeness check stops at the first `//`, so multi-line input containing a comment never completes (verified); `naab-gov lint` is the one channel that shows a HARD block unsanitized with guidance; package-manager errors are one-line `Error:` messages on STDERR, where `run` puts fatal errors on stdout. **Pass-2 leads resolved:** `--quiet` after `run` exits 4 "Unknown flag" and before `run` removes only the `Loaded:` banner, 1 of 33 stderr lines (verified); `ErrorSanitizer::setMode()` is dead -- `sanitize()` takes its mode as a parameter and its only setter, `ErrorSanitizationGuard`, has no users (traced); key environment-variable NAMES in `trace.api_key_env` and `agent.check()` are documented behaviour, inconsistent with `agent.environment()`'s "don't expose key names" comment -- whether a program can read the VALUE behind the name is A33(b) (traced); challenge-call accounting is in A46. | **three passes; every listed channel run once** | Three passes, each changing something, so the stopping rule is still not met. What a fourth would cover: live provider behaviour (A44(b)), Windows output (the CRLF/encoding shapes in CLAUDE.md apply to every channel here), and `--explain`/debugger output. |
| A39 | **The coding model's own channel damages the help it carries.** Verified 2026-10-15 on both engines unless noted. (a) **The display sanitizer rewrites help text.** `ErrorSanitizer::redactValues()` replaces any `name = "value"` with `name = <redacted>` (`error_sanitizer.cpp:121`), and the fatal catch sites run every message through it: a helper's fix example printed `let msg = <redacted> + name + "!"` (VM, `sprintf` hint), a HARD block printed `Rule: code_quality.no_placeholders = <redacted>` and redacted the delivered guidance text, and a parse error redacted the user's own source line in its excerpt (`let name = <redacted>`). Advisories print to stderr unsanitized, so the same message reads differently at `advisory` and `hard`. MCP `naab_run` returns the same redacted text in `output`. (b) **One detail per rule per run** (decided, `aacade58`): three functions with the same TODO print the full message for the first only; the others appear only as a count ("3 warnings"). The location given is the line where the function STARTS (passed by the compiler), and the message does not name the function. F4's grouped summary compensates for function capabilities only. (c) The two closing lines disagree: `Summary ... 3 warnings` then `-- Governance: PASS (... 0 violations)`. (d) The engines give different help for the same mistake (`sprintf(...)`: a printf-specific hint on the VM, generic help on the tree-walker), and a Python `KeyError` prints `Python execution error:` twice, worded differently per engine, with no help on either. (e) A polyglot block's stderr goes to the parent's STDOUT as `[shell stderr]: ...` while its stdout becomes the block's value. (f) Pass 3: help examples are NAAb code whatever language is checked -- `naab-gov check --language python` on a Python TODO shows `fn validate(x) { ... }` as the fix (verified). **Prior record (found in pass 3):** the same control's `API_KEY` pattern already over-redacts identifiers (`max_tokens_per_run (60) exceeded` renders as `max_<redacted> (60) exceeded`), recorded in `governance-campaign-findings.md` and pinned by `test_limit_attribution.sh` LA-03 to flip when fixed; its candidate fix was held because it LOOSENS a redaction pattern. **That half is fixed** (2026-10-24, owner's decision: require a separator -- `:`, `=`, whitespace or a quote -- between the keyword and the value; `test_error_sanitizer_verr001.sh` T8-T17, LA-03 now asserts the name survives); it did not touch (a). (a) is the assignment pattern of that control, so the same constraint applies. | **open** | (a) is the most damaging: the fix example is the part a model acts on. Options for the owner, constrained by the prior record: exempt text the engine authored (`formatError` help, guidance) from value redaction while keeping it for text that carries runtime values -- this narrows WHERE the control runs rather than loosening a pattern, but needs the split carried to the catch sites. Neither option changes a verdict. (b)-(f) are display decisions. |
| A40 | **The engine's own model calls are unfiltered and reach only some runs.** Verified 2026-10-15 with the loopback stub (`agent_stub.py`, authored fixtures). **Governance voice** (`output.voice`, `631db80a`): on the VM a clean run with warnings calls it once and prints its reply TWICE (`runGovernanceVoice()` prints it, then the summary formatter prints it again); a runtime HARD block calls it and prints once on stderr while the block goes to stdout; a compile-time HARD block never calls it (0 calls, both engines); the tree-walker never calls it (0 calls in all three cases) -- traced: `main.cpp:2837` gives the source to the CLI's `shared_governance`, while the interpreter's end-of-run voice runs on its own engine, whose source is empty. The prompt carries every non-passing check's raw message plus the whole script (observed in the stub's request log) and nothing scans the reply; "Do NOT include bypass instructions" is a line in the prompt. **Agent review** under a `hard` zone: the voicer is called (paid) and its guide discarded -- only the first finding is shown, because `enforce()` throws on it before the print (verified, both engines; an advisory zone shows the guide, the control). No test sets `output.voice`. **Bundle 4 (2026-10-28):** the reply prints ONCE -- `c0d3c5da` made `runGovernanceVoice()` print it because the summary formatter never runs on an error path, and the formatters kept printing it too; the formatters now point to it instead (classified regressed at `c0d3c5da`) -- and it passes `secretFinding()`/`piiFinding()` (decide-only, under the project's `no_secrets`/`no_pii`): a matching guide is withheld, neither printed nor cached, and a cache hit goes through the same scan. A compile-time block reaches the voice (since #325's compile wrapper; A43(e)'s wider wrapper calls it too), a whole-source block does not: `setSource()` follows the whole-source checks, so the prompt never carries a value they blocked -- deliberately left there. One call per engine (`voice_ran_`, a precaution). Test: `tests/governance_v4/test_governance_voice.sh` (VO-01/03/04 fail on master; VO-02 is the arm that refuses the other "print once", dropping the immediate print; VO-05 pins the whole-source boundary). | **partly fixed** -- print once and the reply filter done; open: the PROMPT (raw messages plus the whole script, unfiltered when `no_secrets`/`no_pii` are off or a value escapes the whole-source patterns), the tree-walker (never calls it; audit and off only since Bundle 2), agent review's discarded guide under a `hard` zone, and the bypass filter (paused) | The prompt filter is a decision: withhold the call, or the script, when the prompt matches -- either loses the guide exactly when a violation is a leak. |
| A41 | **A NAAb callback that throws through native code leaves the VM out of step.** Found 2026-10-15; **traced and fixed 2026-10-22**. A callback (sort comparator, `for_each`/`find`, `array.map_fn`/`filter_fn`/`reduce_fn`, an agent tool, a contract golden test) runs in a NESTED `VM::run()` entered from native code, all through `VM::callNaabFunction()`. Two defects: (a) `OP_THROW` and `run()`'s two catch clauses routed an exception to `exception_handlers_.back()` without asking whose handler it was, so a `try` in the CALLER's frame caught the throw and the callback's dispatch loop went on to run the caller's catch block and the rest of the caller with the native code still below it on the C++ stack; (b) `callNaabFunction()` restored frames, stack, stop level, depth and taint only after `run()` RETURNED (since the VM landed, `1dd08e83`; the depth counter `44d5d0cc` copied the shape), so a throw left the callback's frames behind. Observed on `16efa905`, VM only (the tree-walker is clean on every probe): a run that finished its catch block and then died after `main` with `Uncaught exception: boom` (`.sort`, `.sorted`, `array.map_fn/filter_fn/reduce_fn`) or `callNaabFunction: value is not a function` (`.for_each`, `.find`, a throw two frames deep); a throwing agent tool made the script's next `r.get()` dispatch as `agent.get`; a callback catching a throw from a callback it started visited one element of three; after 64 caught callback throws every later callback failed with `Maximum callback nesting depth exceeded`. The sweep found a second defect in the family, both engines: `array.sort(arr, cmp)`/`array.sorted(arr, cmp)` (the module form, `V-UB-001`, `2ceb5ec5`) replaced anything the comparator threw with "comparator must return a number" -- including a HARD governance block, which became catchable and the run exited 0 (observed: a blocked `file.read` inside the comparator, rc 0; the same read direct, rc 3). Contract golden tests were clean (each starts a fresh `run()` on top of the leftover frames), and kept as a regression arm. | **FIXED** | `run()` records a handler floor (`exception_handlers_.size()` at entry) and only handlers above it may catch there; a throw with none leaves `run()` as `VMThrownValue` (thrown value + taint), which the run() that owns the handler delivers as a direct throw would, taint included (V-TAINT-THROW). A `VMException` leaving a nested run keeps its type, so its message carries the trace once. `callNaabFunction()` restores the VM on a throw (frames with upvalues closed, handlers, stack, stop level, depth, taint top). The module sort keeps the comparator's exception and rethrows it after `std::sort` returns, as the VM's inline sort already did, so V-UB-001's no-throw-mid-sort and its return-type check both stand. Test: `tests/vm/test_callback_throw.sh` (51 arms, both engines, the tree-walker as the oracle; red first: 22 subject arms failed on `16efa905`, every control passed). CT-11 (a callback's own try still catches) and CT-18 (V-UB-001's type check) are the controls a too-wide fix fails; CT-16 is taint across the boundary; CT-19 the agent tool (VM only, tree-walker tools are F36). Revert-checked per mechanism (see the PR). Not covered: an `async fn` (its own VM, no desync, but a different catch value: A59). |
| A42 | **Help text that contains `{...}` breaks the `fmt` call that prints it.** Verified 2026-10-15; **fixed 2026-10-22**. `agent.<unknown>()` threw `argument not found` instead of its help on both engines, because the help lists `returns {valid, error}` inside a `fmt::format` string (`agent_impl.cpp`, added by `d88bdae0`); `naab-lang blocks test` aborted with `terminate called ... invalid format string`, exit 134 (`main.cpp`, `main { ... }`, added by `263e32d3`). Both were wrong from the commit that wrote them; nothing in the history chose them. **Why the compiler does not catch it**: fmt checks a literal format string at compile time only under C++20 (its `format_string` constructor is `consteval`); NAAb builds as C++17. Observed: a `-std=gnu++20 -fsyntax-only` pass over both files rejects exactly these two sites before the fix and neither after; over all 221 repo units (about 4 min, 4 cores) it rejects no other format string. That pass cannot be the regression test: 8 units do not compile as C++20 because `logger.h` hands fmt a runtime `std::string`, and for the same reason it never sees the 104 `LOG_*` literals. | **FIXED** | Braces escaped at both sites (and `extract_code`, dispatched but missing, added to the help's "Available functions"). `tools/fmtcheck/fmt_literals.py` (screened: a tokenizer, not a compiler) checks every literal format passed to `fmt::format/print/println/format_to/format_to_n`, `spdlog::*` and `LOG_*` by fmt's rules (escapes, a lone `}`, field syntax, named arguments -- NAAb passes none -- auto/manual mixing, and fields against argument count): 3,032 literals, 0 problems after the fix, the two A42 sites before it; `--cxx20` runs the compiler pass. Test: `tests/self-audit/test_fmt_literals.sh` (FL-01 is the probe-usability floor, FL-03/FL-04 plant the defects back into real files, FL-05/FL-06 the symptom on both engines, FL-07 the control). Revert-checked: the pre-fix sources turn FL-02/03/05/06 red with the controls green; 8 scanner mutants each turn an arm red. Not seen: a format string built at run time (`fmt::runtime`, the logger's own forwarding: 10 calls), and any wrapper other than `LOG_*`/spdlog that forwards a format. |
| A43 | **Channels that lose the message.** (a) **Tree-walker fatal errors drop the grouped advisory summary** (verified: the undeclared-effects summary printed on the VM, not on the tree-walker, same program). Traced: the `Interpreter` is declared inside the `try` (`main.cpp:1717`); unwinding destroys it before the catch body runs and its destructor clears the current engine (`interpreter.cpp:693`), so `getCurrent()` is null at the catch sites. CLAUDE.md's "main.cpp now flushes at all four catch sites" holds on the VM only. (b) **MCP `naab_propose` gives no reason for a block** (verified: verdict `blocked`, exit 3, `findings: []`, the message absent from the whole response): it runs `--lint-only` and returns only stderr, while a HARD block prints on stdout. (c) MCP `findings` keeps the first 60 non-empty stderr lines, so the end-of-run groups, printed last, are cut first (traced). (d) **The LSP lost rule codes** (traced): `tools/naab-lsp/document_manager.cpp:248` looks for `Rule (govern.json): `, which `7ab3fa77` deliberately renamed to `Rule: ` (a config-leak fix, 2026-06-16); every governance diagnostic's code has been `governance` since. Pass 3 drove the LSP over stdio and verified it: code `governance`, line 0, and the message is the tail of the `[governance] Loaded:` banner (the config's absolute path) plus the block's first line -- no help, example or guidance. (e) **Pass 3: a compile-time HARD block on the VM writes no report files** -- `run --governance-sarif X --governance-report Y` on a static block produced neither file, while the tree-walker wrote both and a runtime HARD block on the VM wrote SARIF (verified). Traced: the VM compiles at `main.cpp:~2450`, outside the catch that writes reports, so the block reaches the top-level handler, which does not write them. The default engine's SARIF -- the CI code-scanning channel -- therefore omits every static block. Where reports ARE written, they keep every occurrence (both TODO sites), unlike stderr; SARIF's message is the first line only and the JSON report carries the help; neither carries guidance. | **(a) unreachable under enforce since Bundle 2 (`--tree-walk` is refused, exit 4: `tests/cli/test_tree_walk_enforce.sh`); open under audit and off. (b)-(e) FIXED 2026-10-28 (Bundle 4), each red first on master:** (b) `naab_propose` builds `findings` from the worker's stdout and stderr, through the scan `naab_run` uses (one helper, `withholdFindings()`); the new route carries a parse error's source line, which prints a bare-number SSN raw, and is withheld under `no_pii` (MF-04). (c) `findings` keeps the first 20 and last 60 lines and counts the gap: 80 lines of program stderr had pushed out every governance line, the per-occurrence advisory too (observed, not only the end-of-run group). (d) the writer and the LSP share `include/naab/governance_message_format.h` (labels, header detection, rule name without its configured value); the splitter now recognises the CLI's `Error: ` prefix, so the message is the block's own. CI built no `naab-lsp`, so no LSP suite had ever run there; the three Linux jobs build it now. Line is still the `At:` line (a function's start line for a placeholder, as pass 2 recorded). (e) `main.cpp` wraps the whole static phase (whole-source checks, intent preflight, agent review, compile) in write-reports-then-rethrow. Tests: `tests/mcp/test_mcp_findings.sh`, `tests/lsp/test_lsp_governance_diag.sh`, `tests/cli/test_static_block_reports.sh` (kill tables in each header). | (a) flush from an engine that outlives the interpreter, or before the interpreter's destructor. |
| A44 | **The agent tool loop's secret/PII redaction is dead, and a refused tool call leaves an orphan result.** (a) Traced through code and history: `checkSecrets()`/`checkPii()` return `enforce(...)`, and `enforce()` returns `""` or throws, so every `if (!err.empty())` after them is unreachable -- the `[REDACTED: tool result contained potential secrets|PII]` replacement, `Error: arguments contain potential secrets`, and the `AGENT_TOOL_SCAN_HIT` telemetry. They were live when written (`2d3a9d64`, 2026-06-03, when `enforce()` returned the message at HARD/SOFT) and went dead with `fd97d0ae` (2026-06-06, HARD made uncatchable). Since then a hit ends the run (no override) or passes unredacted (`--governance-override`, as before). Direction: stricter than documented, never looser; CLAUDE.md's "result scan" layer describes the redaction. Not run: reproducing needs secret-shaped content. (b) Traced: the five refusal branches in the tool loop (`agent_impl.cpp:3457-3582`) `continue` before the call is added to the assistant message, so the next request carries a tool result with no matching tool call -- a shape the Anthropic API rejects. Not checked against a live provider; the stub does not validate it. | **fixed 2026-10-28** (Bundle 4.2/4.3) | Was: (a) decide: redact-and-continue (restore the intent, with a decide-only scan as guidance uses) or end-the-run (delete the dead branches and correct the docs); (b) add the refused call to the assistant message before its result, and give the stub a pairing check. **Fixed 2026-10-28.** (a) end-the-run, as the plan decided (the stricter of the two; the original intent, `2d3a9d64`, was redact-and-continue): the dead branches are deleted -- the tool loop's argument and result redaction, the prompt and response `throw`s after the scans, and `agent.propose()`'s candidate check, whose comment claimed a scan hit marks the candidate inadmissible while it ended the run (left ending it: marking would hand the secret to the program). `AGENT_TOOL_SCAN_HIT` is gone (emitted only there), and `AGENT_TOOL_RESULT`'s always-false `redacted` field. (b) never built: every refusal now goes through `refuseCall()`, which adds the call to the assistant turn beside its error result, with the `name` Gemini needs. Measured on master with the pairing-checking stub (observed): `responses ['gemini_0'] answer calls []` -- the orphan, and the result's name fallen back to the call id; `agent.send()` returned empty content, silently. Tests: `test_tool_call_pairing.sh` (TP-00..03, TS-01/01c) and `test_tool_admissibility_gate.sh` B-04; red first on master (TP-01/02/03, B-04); three mutants. The unparseable-arguments refusal is unreachable through either provider (both re-serialize parsed arguments) -- traced only. |
| A45 | **REST `/execute` skips every per-function governance check, and the verdict follows a file on the server's disk.** Found 2026-10-15 in message-map pass 3. The tree-walker's per-function checks (`visit(FunctionDecl)`, `interpreter.cpp:1150-1176`) and its whole-source check (`visit(Program)`, `interpreter.cpp:~920-934`) re-read the source from `current_file_` ON DISK. REST names every request `cwd + "/api-request.naab"` (`rest_api.cpp:134`, from `cc975e33` Finding C, 2026-04-04, chosen so config discovery runs from the server's directory), and that file does not exist -- so placeholders, contracts (`must_call` and siblings), oversimplification, complexity floor, incomplete logic and the rest never run for submitted code; only secrets and PII run, through Finding C's separate `checkSecrets`/`checkPii` call. Verified with a matched control, same config (`no_placeholders: hard`): CLI `run` blocks the TODO program at exit 3 on both engines; REST runs it (exit 0, output `3`). With a TODO file planted as `api-request.naab` in the server's directory, REST blocks a CLEAN submission (exit 3); with a clean file planted there, the TODO submission passes. The config loaded (`[governance] Loaded:` in the server log), so this is not a missing config. Classification: never built -- the disk re-read (`247e229d`, EVA-8) predates REST getting a governance context. Adjacent and already recorded: REST never runs agent review (A29, traced). | **fixed** 2026-10-19 | Give the checks the submitted text instead of a path -- the parsed source is in hand at both call sites -- which also removes the CLI tree-walker's re-read (it checks the file on disk, not necessarily the program it parsed). Test: a REST arm per check family with the CLI as the control, plus the planted-file arms above as the regression signature. **FIXED 2026-10-19 (verified, re-measured at `a1e49f3d` first):** REST ran the placeholder, incomplete-logic, oversimplification, `must_call` and main{}-placeholder programs at exit 0 while `run --tree-walk` refused each at exit 3, and both planted-file arms flipped (observed). The checks now read the PARSED text through one helper, `include/naab/declaration_source.h`: the interpreter records each file's text as it parses it (`setSourceCode()`, and the `import`, plugin and `use` loaders) and looks it up by `current_file_`; the VM compiler takes it through `setSourceText()` (main.cpp, `naab-gov lint`, `VM::importModule`). REST's own secrets/PII call is gone -- `visit(Program)` runs them on the submitted text. libnaab's `Context::eval` (default name `<embedded>`) had the same hole and gets the same fix (traced; `tests/unit/declaration_source_test.cpp` `NotOnDisk.*` drives the interpreter the way it does). A file the engine never parsed is still read from disk, as before. Three defects in the same lines were found while fixing it: A47-A49. Not covered: agent review (A29), drift detection and the intent preflight's whole-program part (orphaned `function_intents` keys) still run in the CLI only -- per-function intent now runs in REST through `visit(FunctionDecl)`; the REPL has no governance at all. Test: `tests/governance_v4/test_declaration_source.sh` R arms (R-07/R-08 are the planted-file signature). |
| A46 | **`agent_dispatch.hard_stop.max_tokens_per_run` does not see tool-loop calls, and step-up challenge calls bypass both run budgets.** Found 2026-10-15 resolving a pass-2 lead. Verified with the loopback stub (authored token counts): one `agent.send()` whose tool loop made three provider calls (100+700, 200+700, 300+7 tokens) reported `agent.usage()` 600/1407, while `agent.dispatch_status().tokens_used` -- the counter `max_tokens_per_run` compares -- read 800, the first call only. Traced: the run total is updated at `agent_impl.cpp:2996` (the send's first call) and `5896` (propose) and nowhere else. The step-up challenge call (`agent_impl.cpp:~2418`) increments `s_dispatch.total_calls` directly instead of through `chargeCallBudget()`, so it is counted but never trips `max_calls_per_run` itself, and its tokens go to telemetry only (traced, not run). **`agent.propose()` has the challenge call's shape** (added 2026-10-16): it increments `s_dispatch.total_calls` by hand (`agent_impl.cpp:5871`) once per candidate and never compares it with `max_calls_per_run` -- it checks only `hard_stopped` before generating. Verified with the loopback stub: under `max_calls_per_run: 1`, one `agent.propose(h, msg, 3)` made 3 provider calls and `agent.dispatch_status()` read `calls_made=3, hard_stopped=false`; the control, two `agent.send()` under the same config, refused the second and read `hard_stopped=true`. Found with `tools/codemap` (the callers of each provider function -- `callAgentWithStatus`, `callAgentWithTools`, `callAgentSimple`, `callAgentMultiTurn` -- set against the callers of `chargeCallBudget()`), which also lists the engine's own calls through `callAgentSimple` (governance voice, agent review, A40) as charged to neither budget -- they run outside the program, so whether they belong under a run budget is a decision, not recorded as a defect here -- and `callAgentMultiTurn` as having no caller. Direction: loosening -- a run can spend past the operator's token cap through tool loops, and past its call cap through propose. | **fixed** 2026-10-19 | Re-reproduced on `f7015394` with the loopback stub, counting the requests the stub RECEIVED (observed): all four call sites spent past the caps, including two the row had not named -- the final tool-less turn's tokens (20 of 120 charged) and propose never checking `max_tokens_per_run` (120 spent against a cap of 50) -- and the challenge, traced only above, verified (cap 1: a second request; 800 challenge tokens uncharged, so the send after it ran). History: `chargeCallBudget()` was built by #236 (F54) for the CALL budget of the send and tool-loop paths only; the token half and the challenge/propose sites were never built, not removed. Fix: `chargeTokenBudget()` beside it, every site through both; `chargeCallBudget()` also refuses once the run is hard-stopped (a token or time stop set after an earlier call) and no longer counts a refused call. **The third budget had the same gap**, found while writing this up (`codemap writers DispatchCounters::total_agent_time_ms`: one writer, the send path) and reproduced with replies the stub holds 400 ms each: under `max_agent_time_ms: 600` a 3-call tool loop was charged 402 ms and ran to the end, 3 propose candidates were charged 0, and a challenge call went uncharged; fixed the same way (`chargeTimeBudget()`, every attempt, failed ones too). Test: `tests/governance_v4/test_run_budget_coverage.sh` -- 18 arms, 5 controls; on the pre-fix binary the 13 bypass arms fail and the controls pass; on the commit before the time fix only the 4 time arms fail; reverting only the stopped-run refusal fails exactly RB-05/RB-07, only the refused-call count exactly RB-08, and each time-charging site exactly its own arms (challenge RB-11, tool loop RB-09/09b, propose RB-10). **Still open, a decision:** `consecutive_failure_limit` counts only the send's own attempts (traced: `consecutive_failures` has two writers, both on the send path) -- whether tool-loop, challenge and propose failures belong in the streak, and whether their successes may reset it (that would loosen the breaker for sends), is not settled by symmetry. Left by decision: the engine's own `callAgentSimple()` calls (governance voice, agent review, A40) are charged to neither budget; `callAgentMultiTurn` has no caller. **Measured 2026-10-20 (observed, loopback stub, scripted HTTP 500s, `consecutive_failure_limit: 2`, `retry.max_attempts: 1`):** four `agent.propose(h, m, 2)` whose eight provider calls all failed left `consecutive_failures` at 0 and the run unstopped; four sends whose every tool-loop call failed (each send's first call succeeds, then the loop's call fails, `tool_loop_exit_reason: api_error`) also left it at 0. Control: two failed sends stop the run after 2 requests. The unit is already decided: #184 (`b05b7732`) made one failed SEND one failure -- 'what the key reads as or what an operator sizes it against'. By that unit a propose whose every candidate failed is one failure, and one that got any reply resets the count; a challenge belongs to its send and is not charged separately, as #184 refused to charge retries separately. Still open: whether a send that ends in `api_error` from its tool loop counts as failed (judged by how the operation ends) -- read call by call, success and failure alternate there and are not consecutive. |
| A47 | **On the VM, `fn stub (x)` -- a space before the paren -- made the per-function checks read the PREVIOUS function's body.** Found 2026-10-19 while fixing A45. `7d4fda0f` (VM only) searched up to five lines back for the exact text `fn NAME(` and, when it was absent, started five lines early anyway, so brace counting began inside the function above and stopped at its closing brace. Both directions, verified at `a1e49f3d` with controls: a TODO in `fn stub (x)` after another function ran at exit 0 under a hard `no_placeholders` (the same file with `fn stub(x)` was refused, and the tree-walker refused both); and a `fn needs_sqrt (x)` that calls `math.sqrt` was REFUSED under its `must_call` contract, because the body checked did not. | **fixed** 2026-10-19 | The search now matches `fn` + whitespace + the name as a whole word (whatever follows it), and when no line in the window declares the function it starts at the AST line (`declaresFunction()` / `declarationText()` in `include/naab/declaration_source.h`). Test: `test_declaration_source.sh` V-01 (V-01c/V-01t controls) and V-02; unit `DeclarationSource.SpaceBeforeParenReadsThisFunction`. |
| A48 | **The VM never ran the per-function checks on the main block's body.** Found 2026-10-19 while fixing A45. `247e229d` (EVA-8, "Add MainBlock scanning") added the main{} scan to the tree-walker; `d4f3fa89` brought the function-body checks to the VM ("VM-compiled code skipped all function body quality checks") and did not port the main{} one, and nothing records a reason. Tightening on the default engine: a project with a placeholder or heuristic check on now has main blocks refused that ran before -- as they always were under `--tree-walk`. **It exposed a second, older defect:** `checkNaabFunctionBody()` scopes its location only for an imported module (#300) and leaves an ENTRY-file location set, so every runtime row after an entry-file function was checked looked like a located static one and `deduplicate_checks` collapsed it -- `test_dedup_runtime.sh` passed only because its fixtures have no functions, and went red (DC-01/03/04) once main{} was checked. Verified on a build with the main check and the new scoping both removed (master's shape for this): a function plus four reads gives 1 row instead of 2. The five call sites (both engines' function and main{} visitors) now scope the location around the checks, so the tree-walker's intent check after the body check keeps its location. **And a third:** a refusal from these checks while the VM COMPILES wrote no report -- `main.cpp`'s write-reports-then-rethrow wrapped only `execute()`, while the tree-walker runs the same checks inside its own wrapped `execute()` -- so `--governance-sarif` and friends were silently missing whenever a function body was refused on the VM (observed: exit 3, no SARIF; the tree-walker wrote it), and `tests/cli/test_report_formats.sh` went 2/30 once main{} was checked (its fixture's TODO sits in main). The compile call now has the same wrapper. Test: `test_declaration_source.sh` M-01 (M-01t, M-01c controls), L-01 (L-01c control) and W-01 (W-01t control). |
| A49 | **On the tree-walker, a `}` inside a string or a comment ended the function body the per-function checks read.** Found 2026-10-19 while fixing A45. `11146ceb` taught only the VM's copy of the brace counter to skip strings and comments. Verified at `a1e49f3d`: `let s = "}"` followed by a TODO ran at exit 0 under `--tree-walk` and was refused at exit 3 on the VM; without the brace the tree-walker refused it too. REST runs the tree-walker, so with A45 alone fixed this would have been one string away from the same hole. | **fixed** 2026-10-19 | One brace counter for both engines (`declarationText()`, the VM's string- and `//`-aware one). Test: `test_declaration_source.sh` T-01/T-02 (T-01c, T-01n controls); unit `DeclarationSource.BraceInStringDoesNotEndTheBody`. |
| A50 | **The BSD pre-check's "already recorded" mark outlived the call that set it, and swallowed the next unrelated event.** Found 2026-10-20 while fixing A22. #195 (`b0ed96a9`) made `checkPreExecution()` mark the event it records when a match is let through (ADVISORY), so the post-call emit does not record the same action twice; the mark is a one-shot thread-local that the NEXT `emitEvent()` consumes, whatever it is. It assumed the action's own record always follows. It does not when the action records nothing: it threw (a `file.read` of a missing file, an `agent.send` refused before its own emit), or nothing recorded it (`file.read_lines`, pre-checked and never emitted). Verified at `2e9c120d` with a hard `[decode, file.write]` pattern behind an advisory one completed by the pre-checked call: the decode after it was dropped and the write ran, exit 0, for `file.read_lines`, for a `file.read` that threw and was caught, and for an `agent.send` that threw (there the swallowed event was the same type, a tool re-registration's AGENT_SEND); the same programs with a plain `file.read`, or without the failing call, were refused at exit 3. | **fixed** 2026-10-20 | `GovernanceEngine::endPreExecutionScope()` clears the mark; both VM routes call it once the pre-checked call has returned or thrown (`try`/`catch (...)` around `callStdlibMethod`), so a mark can no longer outlive its call. `checkPreExecution()` has one caller, `VM::run` (code map `callers`, confirmed by grep), so that is every site. Test: `test_bsd_stdlib_events.sh` S-01/S-02/S-03 (S-01c, S-03c controls). |
| A51 | **On the VM, a stdlib function named without parentheses is CALLED, through a route that kept almost none of the call-site governance -- a function granted no action read the whole environment.** Found 2026-10-20 while fixing A22. `OP_GET_MEMBER` on a stdlib module calls the member with no arguments (meant for `math.PI`; `env.get_all` returns the environment, while the tree-walker returns a string). `3984fe9b` (Finding D) gave it taint; the function-attribution sync and BSD came later and reached only `OP_CALL_METHOD`. Verified at `2e9c120d`: under `capabilities.functions` (hard) with `harvest` declaring no actions, `env.get_all()` inside `harvest()` was refused at exit 3, and `env.get_all` without parentheses in the same function returned 147 variables at exit 0 -- the checks inside the call attributed it to a stale stack; and it recorded no BSD event. Traced, not changed: of the other call-site checks the route skips, `checkDangerousCall` has no NAAb-language pattern, and the filesystem and network gates need an argument the route never passes (`env.load_dotenv()` gates its default path itself). | **fixed** 2026-10-20 | The route now syncs the attribution stack and runs the BSD pre-check and record through the A22 table. Its call semantics (calling instead of returning a function) are unchanged and still differ from the tree-walker. Test: `test_bsd_stdlib_events.sh` F-01 (F-01c, F-01s controls), B-02, E-16. **Corrected 2026-10-22 (roadmap item 6, verified):** "the filesystem and network gates need an argument the route never passes" held for the PATH check only. The filesystem MODE gate takes no argument, and OP_CALL_METHOD runs it for a zero-argument call. Probed by running every function of the gated modules (74, from the A22 table) both ways under `filesystem.mode: "read"`, network off: each write or network function fails its argument check on this route before acting, and the one gated function that runs with zero arguments is `env.load_dotenv`, which reads `.env`. Under `blocked_paths: [".env"]` its own default-path check refused it at exit 3. Under `filesystem.mode: "none"` only the sandbox refused it, as an error a NAAb `try/catch` caught (exit 0, program continued), where `env.load_dotenv()` is a HARD block. Nothing was read either way: the defect is the tier. Fixed by moving OP_CALL_METHOD's module gates into one helper, `VM::stdlibCallGates`, called by both routes (tightening only; `file.write` without parentheses is now a HARD refusal under a read-only mode rather than an argument error). Found by enumerating the routes rather than the names (`tools/codemap/predispatch_enum.py`). Test: `test_bsd_stdlib_events.sh` G-01/G-02 (G-01c, G-01s, G-02c controls; G-01 and G-02 fail on the pre-fix build, controls pass). |
| A52 | **`restrictions.polyglot_output: "json"` was checked on one path: the tree-walker's parallel group, which only javascript takes.** Found 2026-10-20 fixing A18; **fixed 2026-10-21**. Measured at `a1252222` and again at `dd7744fd` (observed, `tests/governance_v4/test_foreign_result_checks.sh`): a block returning `"not json"` under `polyglot_output: "json"` ran on the VM in javascript and python, through a persistent runtime's `.exec()` and through `codegen.run()` on both engines, through a tree-walker block-library call, and on the tree-walker in python (all exit 0). On the tree-walker in javascript it was refused (HARD). History: `checkPolyglotOutput()` arrived in `bcf647a0` with no caller, and `2055ed39` ("parallel path feature parity") wired the parallel path only. The sequential path and the VM never had it: never built, not decided. | **fixed** | `GovernanceEngine::checkForeignResult()` runs the A18 output cap and then the format check, and every route A18 wired calls it: the VM inline site and runtime `.exec()` (`foreignResultError()`); the VM block-call sites, which are unreachable; both tree-walker polyglot paths; its runtime `.exec()` and block-library calls; and `codegen.run()` (a byte-count overload). Semantics are unchanged from the one path that checked: a STRING result must parse as JSON, a number, list or dict passes, and the refusal is HARD. Both config forms reach it, the string and `{"format": "json"}`. No config in the tree sets `"json"` (every JSON config parsed), so nothing that runs today is newly refused. Still inert, as `docs/findings/inert-config-keys.md` records: the struct's `max_size`, `validate_json` and `require_structured`, and `format: "string"`. |
| A53 | **An operator hook started by a violation runs in the program's environment: a bare command name is looked up through the live `PATH`.** Found 2026-10-20 tracing A22(ii). `fireHook()` (`governance_reports.cpp`) resolves a command with no `/` by walking `getenv("PATH")` when the hook fires, and the child inherits the process environment minus three NAAb keys; `env.set` writes the real environment, and env writes are allowed by default. The load-time environment snapshot built for operator egress (#312/#313, `include/naab/env_snapshot.h`) is not applied to hooks. | **traced, not demonstrated** | The #313 class: a program steering the operator's own action through the environment. Not measured; no demonstration was built. The remedy the snapshot rule already gives: start hooks from the load-time snapshot, as command guidance sources are, and resolve a bare name against the snapshot's `PATH`. CLAUDE.md records that hooks keep `blocked_read` variables, a decision about values; nothing records that a hook should see variables the program set. |
| A54 | **A role with `shell_allowed: false` still runs `process.run`.** Found 2026-10-20 measuring A37. | **fixed (Bundle 3)** | `--agent-id worker` with `agents.worker.shell_allowed: false`, no reload involved: `<<shell>>` refused (exit 3), `process.run("/bin/echo", ...)` ran (exit 0). CLAUDE.md scopes the per-role gate (`checkShellAllowed()`) to `<<shell>>` blocks and prerequisite commands, and `process.run` answers to the sandbox's SYS_EXEC and the project's `capabilities.shell`; whether a shell-less ROLE should also withhold `process.run` is a decision. A37 keeps this exactly as it is across a reload (RS-02): the reload's sandbox sync reads the shell decision from before the role is laid over, as startup does. **Fixed (Bundle 3, 2026-10-27; plan item 3.2, which the owner approved):** `process.run` calls `checkShellAllowed()` -- the role's `shell_allowed`, its action matrix (`SHELL_EXEC`) and function-scope `SHELL_EXEC` -- before `checkShellCommandAllowed()`; tightening only. Test: `tests/governance_v4/test_process_run_role_gate.sh` (8 arms; PR-01/02/03 red on the pre-fix binary, the controls green). |

The taint mark is the one to measure first. Tree-walker only, which is being phased out (`docs/plan-mcp-service.md`). **It also runs no reload check** (observed 2026-10-21 by A37's suite: a no-op reload swapped in before a javascript block was never seen on the tree-walker; traced: `reloadIfChanged()`'s only tree-walker call is the sequential path, `polyglot.cpp:159`), so a tightening reload does not apply to javascript blocks there until a sequential block runs. **Since A52/A56 (2026-10-21) the parallel path runs the output format check and the output baseline**, through the same engine calls as the sequential path. | A56 | **Output baselines did not work end to end on the CLI.** Found 2026-10-21 building A37's suite; **fixed 2026-10-21**. The CLI already left through `_exit()` at that commit (22 sites), which skips the destructor. Observed at `dd7744fd`: a `baselines: {enabled, auto_record}` run and a `--governance-record-baselines` run wrote no `.naab/baselines.json` on either engine. That is lost, not decided. `docs/findings/claude-md-audit.md` (section C) had traced both halves without a positive control. (b) **The VM checked no baseline**: `checkBaseline()`'s one caller was the tree-walker's sequential path (code map, screened), and the parallel path had none (A55). A planted baseline went unchecked on the VM, and on the tree-walker in javascript. | **fixed** | `writeReports()` now saves recorded baselines. Every CLI exit path calls it, and so does the end of a clean run on both engines. The destructor still saves for an engine that is destroyed. `GovernanceEngine::checkOutputBaseline()` builds the key once (language, the hash of the block's own text, its line) and is called from the VM inline site and from both tree-walker paths. A baseline recorded on one engine is therefore the one the other checks (BL-05, both directions). The store is locked (`baselines_mutex_`), because the VM's async fn workers share the engine. A mismatch is enforced after the lock is released. Not covered: other routes (runtime `.exec()`, `codegen.run()`, block calls) have no baseline, as before. The key's hash is `std::hash`, so a baselines file is portable only between builds of one standard library. The file is not protected from the program, unlike the evidence files (the engine's STATE files are the open follow-up of the evidence-file row). `scoring_calibration.auto_save` has the same destructor-only save (traced, not measured). |

A plain `make` builds again (observed). **Still open, the same class:** 14 more test executables in `all` compile but nothing runs them (`test_profiler`, `test_block_registry`, `test_enhanced_metadata`, `test_search_index`, `test_error_reporting`, `test_examples`, `test_composition_validator`, `test_cross_language*` (4), `test_library_detection`, `test_exception_handling`); and `naab_testing` (BlockTester) is linked into no binary -- its only caller was the deleted program. Both are an owner's call: run, fold, or delete. |
| A59 | **Two VM/tree-walker parity gaps the A41 callback sweep found.** Observed 2026-10-22, not fixed. (a) A throw inside an `async fn`, caught around its `await`, arrives on the VM as a message dict (`{"message": "Await error: async fn work failed\n  Cause: Uncaught exception: async-boom ..."}`, `OP_AWAIT`'s `catch (std::exception)` re-raising through `runtimeError`) and on the tree-walker as the thrown value (`async-boom`). No desync: the async body runs on its own VM. (b) The tree-walker has no `.sorted(fn)` method on arrays ("Arrays don't support dot notation: array.sorted"); the VM has one, and `array.sorted(arr, fn)` works on both. | **(a) FIXED 2026-10-29 (Bundle 5)**; (b) open | (a) is the A41 mechanism one boundary further: carrying `VMThrownValue` through the future would deliver the value, but the value's handles belong to the async thread, so it needs the same `deepCopy()` the success path does -- traced, not built. (b) is tree-walker only (being phased out). **(a) fixed:** `OP_AWAIT` rethrows a `VMThrownValue` from the future with its value copied to the awaiting thread and its taint, and `run()` hands it to the catch, as the tree-walker does. It was also LAUNDERING: the message dict was a fresh untainted value whose text held the thrown value, so an `env.get` value thrown from an async fn reached `file.write` unflagged (observed on master). Building it found a crash on the success path too: a closure returned (or now thrown) out of an async fn pointed into the function copies only the worker owned, freed with the future (`basic_string::_M_create`, or SIGSEGV when called); closures made in an async call now share ownership of those copies (`VMClosure::function_owner`), with no cycle (constants never hold it). `tests/vm/test_async_throw.sh`. |
| A60 | **A governed program can lift its own cumulative risk gate.** Found 2026-10-21 by the A64 sweep (`saveScoringCalibration`, called only from the destructor the CLI's `_exit()` skips). Verified at `dd7744fd`, `scoring` and `scoring_calibration` enabled, ten runtime `no_placeholders` advisories through `codegen.run`: without calibration exit 2 ("Cumulative risk score 30 reached threshold 8"); after `governance.calibrate("code_quality.no_placeholders", 0, ...)` exit 0; with `scoring_calibration` off the call returns false and the run exits 2; and a `file.write` of the calibration file before the first violation also exits 0 (the file is read once, at the first violation). Opt-in: the template ships both off; `examples/living-script_v2`, `living-script_extended` and two naab-40 phases (`cat1-calibration`, `cat3-dashboard`) enable both. The template calls it "operator-driven" and naab-40 C7 asserts that weight 0 suppresses a rule, so a program calibrating itself looks intended; `docs/security-decisions.md` rejected `governance.resign()` for the same shape (an in-process route that weakens a running gate without the out-of-band signing step). | **fixed** (owner's decision 2026-10-22: raise-only) | `calibrateRule()` refuses a weight below the rule's current one -- its override if it has one, else the highest configured weight over the rule's contexts (each `rule:...` contextual key, plus the plain key or, without one, the default), since an override replaces the weight in every context. The refusal is `{success: false, refused: ...}` plus one stderr line. Lowering stays the operator's: they edit the calibration file outside the run, and the program may no longer WRITE that file (`governance.state_files`, HARD; reads allowed; the evidence-file mechanism, one shared path matcher; subprocess languages do not reach it, CONTRA-013). Load, save and the protection resolve the path through one helper (`scoringCalibrationFile()`): save had used the path as written, so beside a telemetry file in another directory a saved calibration was never read back (traced). Test: `tests/governance_v4/test_calibration_raise_only.sh` (11 arms; 5 red on the pre-fix binary -- the lowering, the contextual floor, both write routes, the resolved path -- with every control green; the contextual floor and the path helper each revert-checked alone). naab-40 changed with it: A5 (a negative weight was clamped to 0) now asserts the refusal, A10 calibrates up, and C7's weight 0 is an operator file the harness writes, with a new control that DASH-TRIGGER fired (C7 goes red without the file). `examples/living-script_extended` now raises instead of lowering. Still open: the engine's other state files (output baselines, A56) are not protected from the program, and calibration is still saved only by the destructor the CLI's `_exit()` skips. |

Not investigated further. | **FIXED 2026-10-29 (Bundle 5)** | Trace where the async worker gets its QuickJS context (the async polyglot pool carries only the sandbox -- see CLAUDE.md, child-environment gotcha). **Fixed:** the one QuickJS runtime was made on the main thread and records that thread's stack as its limit, so on any other thread every evaluation failed its stack check before the error could be formatted (reproduced in a standalone QuickJS program: the runtime works on its own thread, fails on another, works there after `JS_UpdateStackTop` or when made on that thread). A runtime is not safe to share either. `JsExecutorAdapter` now runs a call from any thread but its own on a fresh executor made on that thread (about 0.3 ms, measured), as the tree-walker's parallel pool does, and keeps the last call's exit code and error text per thread. `tests/vm/test_async_javascript.sh`, unit `JsExecutorAdapterTest`. |
